- [Get outgoing transaction count](https://docs.tatum.io/reference/gettransactioncountblockchainsapi.md): <p style='display: none'>/v4/data/blockchains/transaction/count</p> <p><b>10 credits per API call</b></p>  <p>Get the count of outgoing transactions (including pending) for the given address.</p>
- [Get block hash by block number](https://docs.tatum.io/reference/getblockhashblockchainsapi.md): <p style='display: none'>/v4/data/blockchains/block/hash</p> <p><b>10 credits per API call</b></p>  <p>Get the block hash for a given block height (block number) for supported blockchains. Use block number only (e.g. ledger index for Ripple, sequence for Stellar).</p>
- [Get UTXO info by tx hash and index](https://docs.tatum.io/reference/getutxoinfoblockchainsapi.md): <p style='display: none'>/v4/data/blockchains/utxo/info</p> <p><b>10 credits per API call</b></p>  <p>Get information about a transaction output (UTXO) by transaction hash and output index. Supported for Bitcoin, Litecoin, Dogecoin, and Bitcoin Cash. Response is normalized: <code>value</code> in smallest unit (satoshis), <code>address</code>, <code>script</code>, <code>hash</code>, <code>index</code>, and <code>height</code> (block height; for mempool/unconfirmed UTXOs, <code>height</code> is <code>-1</code>). If the output is spent, the API returns 403.</p>
- [Get mempool transaction hashes](https://docs.tatum.io/reference/getmempoolblockchainsapi.md): <p style='display: none'>/v4/data/blockchains/mempool</p> <p><b>20 credits per API call</b></p>  <p>Get mempool (unconfirmed) transaction hashes for a UTXO chain. Supported chains: Bitcoin, Litecoin, Dogecoin, BCH (mainnet and testnet). Response is an array of transaction hashes (strings).</p>
- [Get transaction history (UTXO)](https://docs.tatum.io/reference/gettransactionhistoryutxosblockchainsapi.md): <p style='display: none'>/v4/data/blockchains/transaction/history/utxos</p> <p><b>20 credits per API call</b></p>  <p>Get transaction history by address for UTXO chains (Bitcoin, Litecoin, Dogecoin, Cardano) with pagination and optional block range and tx type filter.</p>  <p><b>Cardano (ADA):</b> Only <code>address</code>, <code>pageSize</code>, and <code>offset</code> are supported. Filters <code>blockFrom</code>, <code>blockTo</code>, and <code>txType</code> are not available for Cardano.</p>
- [Get transaction history (UTXO) batch](https://docs.tatum.io/reference/gettransactionhistoryutxosbatchblockchainsapi.md): <p style='display: none'>/v4/data/blockchains/transaction/history/utxos/batch</p> <p><b>20 credits per address (e.g. 5 addresses = 100 credits)</b></p>  <p>Get transaction history by address for multiple addresses in one request (UTXO chains: Bitcoin, Litecoin, Dogecoin, Cardano). Max 30 addresses. Optional <code>txType</code> filter (incoming/outgoing) for BTC/LTC/DOGE.</p>  <p><b>Cardano (ADA):</b> Only <code>addresses</code> are used. Filter <code>txType</code> is not available for Cardano. Returns ADA format (see example).</p>
- [Get internal transactions by address](https://docs.tatum.io/reference/getinternaltransactionsblockchainsapi.md): <p style='display: none'>/v4/data/blockchains/transaction/internal</p> <p><b>20 credits per API call</b></p>  <p>Get internal transactions by address for EVM chains (Ethereum, BSC, Polygon, Celo, Base, Arbitrum, Optimism, Bera, Unichain, Monad).</p>
- [Storage API](https://docs.tatum.io/reference/storage-api.md): Upload files to Walrus decentralized storage, track certification status, list upload jobs, and manage automatic renewals with Tatum’s Storage API.
- [Upload a file to Walrus storage](https://docs.tatum.io/reference/storageupload.md): <p style='display: none'>/v4/data/storage/upload</p> <p><b>Billed via Walrus on-chain storage fees — no standard API credits consumed per call.</b></p>  <p>Upload a file to <a href="https://walrus.space" target="_blank">Walrus</a> decentralized storage. Files are staged in GCS and then certified on-chain by the Walrus worker asynchronously. The response immediately returns a <code>jobId</code> and a pre-computed <code>blobId</code>; poll <code>GET /v4/data/storage/upload/{jobId}</code> to track certification progress.</p>  <p><b>Epochs and renewals:</b> Walrus storage is divided into epochs (approximately 2 weeks each). Files are stored for the platform default of ~4 epochs (~8 weeks) and are <b>automatically renewed</b> by Tatum before each epoch window closes. Renewal fees (SUI/WAL on-chain costs × your plan rate) are charged to your Walrus storage balance. To stop automatic renewal, call <code>DELETE /v4/data/storage/upload/{jobId}</code>.</p>  <ul> <li>Maximum file size: <b>50 MiB</b>.</li> <li>Only available on <b>mainnet</b> API keys.</li> <li>If another active upload already exists with the same filename and size, the filename is automatically disambiguated (e.g. <code>report (1).pdf</code>) to avoid blob-id collisions on-chain.</li> <li>Send the file as a <code>multipart/form-data</code> field named <code>file</code>.</li> </ul>
- [List Walrus storage uploads](https://docs.tatum.io/reference/storagelistuploads.md): <p style='display: none'>/v4/data/storage/uploads</p> <p><b>0 credits per API call</b></p>  <p>List all upload jobs for the current API key, ordered by most recently created. Use <code>limit</code> and <code>offset</code> to paginate through results.</p>
- [Get Walrus storage upload status](https://docs.tatum.io/reference/storagegetuploadstatus.md): <p style='display: none'>/v4/data/storage/upload/{jobId}</p> <p><b>0 credits per API call</b></p>  <p>Get the current status of a Walrus storage upload job. Poll this endpoint after <code>POST /v4/data/storage/upload</code> to track progress through the lifecycle: <code>PENDING</code> → <code>UPLOADING</code> → <code>CERTIFIED</code>.</p>  <p>Once status is <code>CERTIFIED</code>, the response includes <code>downloadUrlByQuiltId</code> and <code>downloadUrlByQuiltPatchId</code> which can be used to retrieve the file from any Walrus aggregator node. The <code>walrusStartEpoch</code> and <code>walrusEndEpoch</code> fields indicate the active storage window (one epoch ≈ 2 weeks).</p>  <p>If status is <code>FAILED</code>, the <code>errorMessage</code> field contains the reason. Failed jobs are not retried automatically — submit a new upload request to try again.</p>
- [Cancel Walrus storage renewal](https://docs.tatum.io/reference/storagecancelrenewal.md): <p style='display: none'>/v4/data/storage/upload/{jobId}</p> <p><b>Soft cancel: 0 credits. Instant delete: billed via Walrus on-chain storage fees (same credit check as upload).</b></p>  <p>Cancel renewal for a certified Walrus storage upload. Two modes are available:</p>  <ul> <li><b>Soft cancel</b> (default, <code>instant=false</code>): marks the blob as <code>noRenewal=true</code>. The file remains on-chain until its current storage epoch expires (<code>walrusEndEpoch</code>), then decommissions naturally. No additional fee is incurred — the remaining prepaid epochs are simply not renewed.</li> <li><b>Instant delete</b> (<code>instant=true</code>): queues an immediate on-chain delete transaction. Requires sufficient Walrus storage credits (same check as upload). The Walrus worker executes the delete tx and the on-chain delete fee is charged to your Walrus storage balance afterward. Can be applied even if soft cancel was already set — it upgrades to instant.</li> </ul>  <p>Both modes are idempotent. The job must be in <code>CERTIFIED</code> status and not already decommissioned.</p>
- [List prediction-market events](https://docs.tatum.io/reference/getpredictioneventsv4.md): <p style='display: none'>/v4/data/prediction/events</p> <p><b>100 credits per API call</b></p>  <p>List prediction-market events across supported platforms. An event is a top-level grouping of one or more markets that share a common question or outcome set (e.g. "2028 US Presidential Election"). Each event row includes a roll-up of nested market counts, total volume, and total liquidity, with an opt-in to embed the full list of nested markets via <code>includeMarkets=true</code>.</p>  <p>Supported platforms (filter via <code>platform</code> or omit for a cross-platform feed):</p> <ul> <li>Polymarket — <code>polymarket</code></li> <li>Kalshi — <code>kalshi</code></li> </ul>  <p><b>Filtering, sorting and pagination</b></p> <ul> <li>The <code>status</code>, <code>category</code>, <code>tag</code> and <code>search</code> filters are only honoured by Polymarket. To use any of them you must also pin <code>platform=polymarket</code> — calling them without that pin returns <code>400</code> so the response is honest about what was filtered.</li> <li><code>sort</code> accepts <code>volume_desc</code>, <code>liquidity_desc</code>, <code>endingSoon</code>, <code>newest</code>. When omitted on a cross-platform feed, results are interleaved so neither platform dominates the first page.</li> <li>Pagination is cursor-based. The first call returns a <code>pagination.cursor</code>; pass that value verbatim as <code>?cursor=…</code> on the next call. Cursors are opaque, fingerprint-bound to the filter set, and expire when filters change — never construct or modify them by hand. The cursor field is omitted from <code>pagination</code> on the last page.</li> </ul>  <p><b>Other notes</b></p> <ul> <li><code>volumeUnit</code> is <code>usd</code> on Polymarket events (dollar-denominated volume) and <code>contracts</code> on Kalshi events (number of contracts traded, not USD).</li> <li><code>totalLiquidity</code> is <code>null</code> on Kalshi — quoted liquidity is not exposed at the event level on Kalshi; compute from per-market orderbook depth if you need it.</li> <li><code>status</code> in the response is the verbatim upstream status string (richer than the <code>status</code> filter enum) — values can include <code>paused</code>, <code>archived</code>, <code>settled</code>, etc.</li> <li><code>markets</code> is populated only when <code>includeMarkets=true</code> is passed; otherwise it is <code>null</code>. When populated, every nested market is returned (no internal cap).</li> </ul>
- [Get a prediction-market event by ID](https://docs.tatum.io/reference/getpredictioneventbyidv4.md): <p style='display: none'>/v4/data/prediction/events/{id}</p> <p><b>100 credits per API call</b></p>  <p>Retrieve a single prediction-market event by its platform-native identifier. Useful for hydrating event detail pages or following links from a listing.</p>  <p>Accepted <code>id</code> formats:</p> <ul> <li>Polymarket — numeric event id (e.g. <code>12345</code>).</li> <li>Kalshi — event ticker (e.g. <code>KXELONMARS</code>). Tickers are uppercase alphanumeric with optional <code>-</code> and <code>_</code> separators.</li> </ul>  <p><b>Other notes</b></p> <ul> <li>Returns <code>404</code> when the event does not exist on either platform.</li> <li>Returns <code>400</code> when the <code>id</code> does not match either accepted format.</li> <li><code>markets</code> is populated only when <code>includeMarkets=true</code> is passed; otherwise it is <code>null</code>.</li> <li><code>volumeUnit</code> and <code>totalLiquidity</code> follow the same per-platform rules as the listing endpoint.</li> </ul>
- [List prediction markets](https://docs.tatum.io/reference/getpredictionmarketsv4.md): <p style='display: none'>/v4/data/prediction/markets</p> <p><b>100 credits per API call</b></p>  <p>List prediction markets across supported platforms. A market is a single binary or multi-outcome question (e.g. "Will candidate X win?"); it belongs to a parent event when one is defined upstream. Each row carries pricing-friendly roll-ups (volume, liquidity), the current outcome set, and on-chain references on platforms where they exist.</p>  <p>Supported platforms (filter via <code>platform</code> or omit for a cross-platform feed):</p> <ul> <li>Polymarket — <code>polymarket</code></li> <li>Kalshi — <code>kalshi</code></li> </ul>  <p><b>Filtering, sorting and pagination</b></p> <ul> <li>The <code>status</code>, <code>category</code>, <code>tag</code>, <code>search</code> and <code>minVolume</code> filters are only honoured by Polymarket. To use any of them you must also pin <code>platform=polymarket</code> — calling them without that pin returns <code>400</code>.</li> <li><code>eventId</code> accepts either a Polymarket numeric event id or a Kalshi event ticker. When omitted, results span all events on the targeted platform(s).</li> <li><code>endingBefore</code> is an ISO-8601 timestamp; markets closing on or after that timestamp are excluded.</li> <li><code>sort</code> accepts <code>volume_desc</code>, <code>liquidity_desc</code>, <code>endingSoon</code>, <code>newest</code>. When omitted on a cross-platform feed, results are interleaved between the two platforms.</li> <li>Pagination is cursor-based. The first call returns a <code>pagination.cursor</code>; pass it back verbatim as <code>?cursor=…</code> on the next call. Cursors are opaque, fingerprint-bound, and expire when filters change — never construct or modify them by hand.</li> </ul>  <p><b>Other notes</b></p> <ul> <li><code>volumeUnit</code> is <code>usd</code> on Polymarket (dollar-denominated notional) and <code>contracts</code> on Kalshi (contract count — not USD).</li> <li><code>liquidity</code> is <code>null</code> on Kalshi — quoted liquidity is not exposed by the upstream markets feed; aggregate orderbook depth client-side if you need it.</li> <li><code>chain</code> is populated for Polymarket markets (Polygon CTF) and <code>null</code> on Kalshi which has no on-chain footprint.</li> <li><code>status</code> in the response is the verbatim upstream status string (richer than the <code>?status=</code> filter enum) — values can include <code>paused</code>, <code>archived</code>, <code>settled</code>, etc.</li> </ul>
- [Get a prediction market by ID](https://docs.tatum.io/reference/getpredictionmarketbyidv4.md): <p style='display: none'>/v4/data/prediction/markets/{id}</p> <p><b>100 credits per API call</b></p>  <p>Retrieve a single prediction market by its platform-native identifier.</p>  <p>Accepted <code>id</code> formats:</p> <ul> <li>Polymarket — condition id (<code>0x</code> + 64 hex characters).</li> <li>Kalshi — market ticker (e.g. <code>KXELONMARS-99</code>). Tickers are uppercase alphanumeric with optional <code>-</code> and <code>_</code> separators.</li> </ul>  <p><b>Other notes</b></p> <ul> <li>Returns <code>404</code> when the market does not exist on either platform.</li> <li>Returns <code>400</code> when the <code>id</code> does not match either accepted format.</li> <li><code>volumeUnit</code>, <code>liquidity</code> and <code>chain</code> follow the same per-platform rules as the listing endpoint.</li> </ul>
- [Get current market price](https://docs.tatum.io/reference/getpredictionmarketpricev4.md): <p style='display: none'>/v4/data/prediction/markets/{id}/price</p> <p><b>100 credits per API call</b></p>  <p>Current price snapshot for a single market. Returns per-outcome quotes (best bid / best ask / sizes), the implied midpoint, spread, last trade price and rolling 24-hour volume. Useful for ticker-style dashboards and pre-trade previews.</p>  <p><b>Other notes</b></p> <ul> <li>Resolves the same <code>id</code> formats as <code>GET /markets/{id}</code> (Polymarket condition id or Kalshi market ticker).</li> <li><code>volume24h</code> is reported in the market's <code>volumeUnit</code> — <code>usd</code> on Polymarket, <code>contracts</code> on Kalshi.</li> <li><code>volume24h</code> and <code>volumeUnit</code> are <code>null</code> when the upstream does not expose a 24-hour figure.</li> <li><code>midPrice</code> and <code>spread</code> are <code>null</code> when either side of the book is empty.</li> </ul>
- [Get current market orderbook](https://docs.tatum.io/reference/getpredictionmarketorderbookv4.md): <p style='display: none'>/v4/data/prediction/markets/{id}/orderbook</p> <p><b>100 credits per API call</b></p>  <p>Current orderbook snapshot for one side of a market. The orderbook is per-outcome — pass <code>side=yes</code> or <code>side=no</code> to choose which outcome's book you want. Returns top-of-book through to <code>depth</code> levels of bids and asks, with a computed quoted-liquidity figure within 1% and 5% of the midpoint.</p>  <p><b>Other notes</b></p> <ul> <li><code>side</code> is required.</li> <li><code>hash</code> is a content fingerprint of the book snapshot useful for ETag-style change detection on poll. Populated on Polymarket; <code>null</code> on Kalshi (no equivalent upstream field).</li> <li>Returns <code>404</code> when the market does not exist on either platform.</li> </ul>
- [Get market price history](https://docs.tatum.io/reference/getpredictionmarketpricehistoryv4.md): <p style='display: none'>/v4/data/prediction/markets/{id}/history</p> <p><b>200 credits per API call</b></p>  <p>Historical OHLC price candles for a market over a time window. Used to power chart views — every candle includes open / high / low / close prices plus the in-bucket trade volume.</p>  <p><b>Filtering</b></p> <ul> <li><code>interval</code> is required. Accepted values: <code>1m</code>, <code>5m</code>, <code>15m</code>, <code>1h</code>, <code>4h</code>, <code>1d</code>, <code>1w</code>. Each interval has a maximum allowed range (e.g. <code>1m</code> can be queried for ~1 day at a time, <code>1d</code> for ~1 year). Requests exceeding the range cap return <code>400</code>.</li> <li><code>start</code> and <code>end</code> are required ISO-8601 timestamps. <code>end</code> must be after <code>start</code>.</li> <li><code>source</code> selects the underlying data feed. Defaults to <code>candlesticks</code> (trade-based OHLC with real volume). Pass <code>midpoint</code> to switch to a midpoint-quote feed with broader range coverage at the cost of <code>volume = 0</code> on every candle. <code>source=midpoint</code> is supported on Polymarket only — passing it against a Kalshi market returns <code>400</code>.</li> </ul>  <p><b>Other notes</b></p> <ul> <li><code>partial: true</code> indicates the requested range was truncated by an upstream availability limit. This only happens on Polymarket with <code>source=candlesticks</code> (the trade-based feed exposes a finite window of historical fills); Kalshi candles cover the full requested range, and <code>source=midpoint</code> on Polymarket also covers the full range.</li> <li>When <code>partial</code> is <code>true</code>, <code>availableFrom</code> contains the earliest timestamp the response actually covers — data before that point is unavailable from the trade-based feed. To recover the missing range on Polymarket, repeat the call with <code>source=midpoint</code>; the response spans the full window at the cost of <code>volume = 0</code> on every candle (the midpoint feed carries quotes, not fills).</li> <li><code>availableFrom</code> is <code>null</code> when no trades exist in the entire requested range.</li> <li>Candle timestamps are bucket-aligned (e.g. an <code>interval=1h</code> response has timestamps on the hour).</li> </ul>
- [Get recent market trades](https://docs.tatum.io/reference/getpredictionmarkettradesv4.md): <p style='display: none'>/v4/data/prediction/markets/{id}/trades</p> <p><b>100 credits per API call</b></p>  <p>Recent trades feed for a single market. Each row is one fill — outcome side, buy/sell direction, price, size and notional value — with the taker wallet attribution when the upstream exposes it.</p>  <p><b>Filtering and pagination</b></p> <ul> <li><code>after</code> and <code>before</code> are ISO-8601 timestamps that define an inclusive time window. They are honoured by Kalshi natively. On Polymarket the upstream <code>/trades</code> feed has no time-window filter, so passing <code>after</code> or <code>before</code> against a Polymarket market returns <code>400</code> — set the platform via the path id rather than attempting cross-platform time queries.</li> <li>Pagination is cursor-based. The first call returns a <code>pagination.cursor</code>; pass it back verbatim as <code>?cursor=…</code> on the next call. Cursors are fingerprint-bound to the time-window filter set.</li> </ul>  <p><b>Other notes</b></p> <ul> <li><code>taker</code> is the wallet that crossed the spread on this fill. Populated on Polymarket; <code>null</code> on Kalshi (the public trades feed does not expose per-trade wallet attribution there).</li> <li><code>txHash</code> is the on-chain settlement transaction hash where one exists. Populated on Polymarket and <code>null</code> on Kalshi.</li> <li><code>side</code> is the outcome side (<code>yes</code> / <code>no</code>); <code>direction</code> is the order side (<code>buy</code> / <code>sell</code>) — both are surfaced because they answer different questions.</li> </ul>
- [Get top holders of a market](https://docs.tatum.io/reference/getpredictionmarketpositionsv4.md): <p style='display: none'>/v4/data/prediction/markets/{id}/positions</p> <p><b>100 credits per API call</b></p>  <p>Top holders of a market grouped by outcome token. Each top-level row in the response is one outcome (e.g. YES, NO); under it sits the list of wallets currently holding that outcome, ordered by the chosen <code>sortBy</code> metric. Useful for "who's on each side of this bet" leaderboards on a market-detail page.</p>  <p><b>Platform support</b></p> <ul> <li>Polymarket only. Calling this endpoint against a Kalshi market id returns <code>400</code>.</li> </ul>  <p><b>Filtering, sorting and pagination</b></p> <ul> <li><code>user</code> narrows the response to a single wallet — useful for "does this user have a position in this market" checks.</li> <li><code>status</code> filters holders by their position state: <code>OPEN</code> (size > 0.01), <code>CLOSED</code> (size ≤ 0.01), <code>ALL</code> (default).</li> <li><code>sortBy</code> chooses the metric used to order holders inside each outcome group: <code>TOKENS</code>, <code>CASH_PNL</code>, <code>REALIZED_PNL</code>, <code>TOTAL_PNL</code>. Combine with <code>sortDirection</code> (<code>ASC</code> or <code>DESC</code>).</li> <li>Pagination is offset-based. <code>limit</code> and <code>offset</code> apply <em>per outcome group</em> — e.g. on a binary market with <code>limit=50</code> the response carries up to 100 holders total (50 YES + 50 NO).</li> </ul>
- [Get wallet portfolio summary](https://docs.tatum.io/reference/getpredictionwalletportfoliov4.md): <p style='display: none'>/v4/data/prediction/wallets/{address}/portfolio</p> <p><b>200 credits per API call</b></p>  <p>Aggregated portfolio summary for a single wallet. Returns position counts (open, redeemable, closed), unrealised and realised PnL, total cost basis, active notional, win rate and ROI — all computed over the requested <code>period</code>. Useful as the headline KPI block on a wallet detail page.</p>  <p><b>Platform support</b></p> <ul> <li>Polymarket only. Passing any non-EVM address returns <code>400</code>.</li> </ul>  <p><b>Filtering</b></p> <ul> <li><code>period</code> selects the lookback window for closed-position aggregates: <code>24h</code>, <code>7d</code>, <code>30d</code> or <code>all</code>. Defaults to <code>all</code>.</li> <li>Open-position counts and <code>activeNotional</code> are always live (the period only affects closed-position roll-ups).</li> </ul>  <p><b>Other notes</b></p> <ul> <li><code>address</code> is normalised to lowercase before being echoed back in the response.</li> <li><code>winRate</code> is a percentage in <code>[0, 100]</code> (e.g. <code>42.5</code> = 42.5%). It is <code>null</code> when <code>closedPositions</code> is zero — the ratio is undefined, not zero.</li> <li><code>roi</code> is realised ROI as a percentage (e.g. <code>23.45</code> = 23.45%). Computed from realised PnL over total cost basis of closed positions only — unrealised PnL is excluded. <code>null</code> when <code>totalCostBasis</code> is zero.</li> <li><code>redeemablePositions</code> is the count of positions on a resolved market that the wallet has won but has not yet redeemed. Disjoint from both <code>openPositions</code> and <code>closedPositions</code> — tracked separately so the count of currently actionable positions is visible.</li> </ul>
- [Get wallet open positions](https://docs.tatum.io/reference/getpredictionwalletpositionsv4.md): <p style='display: none'>/v4/data/prediction/wallets/{address}/positions</p> <p><b>100 credits per API call</b></p>  <p>Current open positions held by a wallet — one row per outcome token, with unrealised PnL, current value, lifetime cost basis, and the parent market's question, slug and outcome metadata. Useful for "what's in my portfolio right now" views and per-market drill-downs.</p>  <p><b>Platform support</b></p> <ul> <li>Polymarket only. Kalshi wallets return <code>400</code>.</li> </ul>  <p><b>Filtering, sorting and pagination</b></p> <ul> <li><code>market</code> scopes the response to a comma-separated list of condition ids (<code>0x</code> + 64 hex), up to 50 entries.</li> <li><code>eventId</code> scopes the response to a comma-separated list of Polymarket numeric event ids, up to 50 entries. Mutually exclusive with <code>market</code> — passing both returns <code>400</code>.</li> <li><code>sizeThreshold</code> filters out positions smaller than the given size. Defaults to <code>1</code>; pass <code>0</code> to include dust.</li> <li><code>redeemable=true</code> returns positions on resolved markets that the wallet has won but has not yet redeemed. <code>mergeable=true</code> returns positions eligible for merging back to USDC. Both default to <code>false</code>.</li> <li><code>sortBy</code> accepts <code>CURRENT</code>, <code>INITIAL</code>, <code>TOKENS</code>, <code>CASHPNL</code>, <code>PERCENTPNL</code>, <code>TITLE</code>, <code>RESOLVING</code>, <code>PRICE</code>, <code>AVGPRICE</code>. Defaults to <code>TOKENS</code>. Combine with <code>sortDirection</code> (<code>ASC</code> / <code>DESC</code>, defaults to <code>DESC</code>).</li> <li><code>title</code> filters by a substring of the market question.</li> <li>Pagination is offset-based — <code>limit</code> (1..500, default 100) and <code>offset</code> (0..10000, default 0).</li> </ul>  <p><b>Other notes</b></p> <ul> <li><code>address</code> is normalised to lowercase before being echoed in the response (every row's <code>proxyWallet</code>).</li> <li><code>negativeRisk</code> indicates whether the parent market is part of Polymarket's negative-risk grouping (e.g. multi-candidate winner-take-all markets).</li> </ul>
- [Get wallet closed positions](https://docs.tatum.io/reference/getpredictionwallethistoryv4.md): <p style='display: none'>/v4/data/prediction/wallets/{address}/history</p> <p><b>100 credits per API call</b></p>  <p>Closed and resolved positions for a wallet — one row per settled position (winning or losing), with the realised PnL, lifetime cost basis, settlement price, and the parent market's question and outcome metadata. Useful for backtest-style P&L drill-downs and tax exports.</p>  <p><b>Platform support</b></p> <ul> <li>Polymarket only. Kalshi wallets return <code>400</code>.</li> </ul>  <p><b>Filtering, sorting and pagination</b></p> <ul> <li><code>market</code> scopes the response to a comma-separated list of condition ids (<code>0x</code> + 64 hex), up to 50 entries.</li> <li><code>eventId</code> scopes the response to a comma-separated list of Polymarket numeric event ids, up to 50 entries. Mutually exclusive with <code>market</code> — passing both returns <code>400</code>.</li> <li><code>title</code> filters by a substring of the market question.</li> <li><code>sortBy</code> accepts <code>REALIZEDPNL</code>, <code>TITLE</code>, <code>PRICE</code>, <code>AVGPRICE</code>, <code>TIMESTAMP</code>. Defaults to <code>REALIZEDPNL</code>. Combine with <code>sortDirection</code> (<code>ASC</code> / <code>DESC</code>, defaults to <code>DESC</code>).</li> <li>Pagination is offset-based — <code>limit</code> (1..50, default 10) and <code>offset</code> (0..100000, default 0).</li> </ul>  <p><b>Other notes</b></p> <ul> <li><code>address</code> is normalised to lowercase before being echoed in the response (every row's <code>proxyWallet</code>).</li> <li><code>timestamp</code> is unix-seconds (not milliseconds) — the settlement time of the position.</li> <li><code>curPrice</code> is the resolution price (0 or 1 for binary markets); <code>avgPrice</code> is the volume-weighted entry price.</li> </ul>
- [Get wallet trade history](https://docs.tatum.io/reference/getpredictionwallettradesv4.md): <p style='display: none'>/v4/data/prediction/wallets/{address}/trades</p> <p><b>100 credits per API call</b></p>  <p>Trade-only fills for a wallet — one row per trade, with the buy/sell side, outcome metadata, size, price, transaction hash, and the trader's profile fields (display name, profile image). Trade-only — does not include non-trade events like splits, merges, redemptions or rewards (use <code>/wallets/{address}/activity</code> for the full feed).</p>  <p><b>Platform support</b></p> <ul> <li>Polymarket only. Kalshi wallets return <code>400</code>.</li> </ul>  <p><b>Filtering and pagination</b></p> <ul> <li><code>market</code> / <code>eventId</code> scope the response (mutually exclusive, up to 50 entries each).</li> <li><code>side</code> filters to <code>BUY</code> or <code>SELL</code> trades.</li> <li><code>takerOnly</code> defaults to <code>true</code> — set to <code>false</code> to include maker-side fills.</li> <li><code>filterType</code> and <code>filterAmount</code> set a minimum trade size threshold. <code>filterType=CASH</code> filters by USDC notional; <code>filterType=TOKENS</code> filters by outcome-token count. Both must be provided together — passing one without the other returns <code>400</code>.</li> <li>Pagination is offset-based — <code>limit</code> (1..10000, default 100) and <code>offset</code> (0..10000, default 0). The upstream feed enforces a hard cap at <code>offset = 3000</code>; calls past that point return an empty page rather than an error.</li> </ul>  <p><b>Other notes</b></p> <ul> <li><code>address</code> is normalised to lowercase before being echoed in the response (every row's <code>proxyWallet</code>).</li> <li><code>timestamp</code> is unix-seconds (not milliseconds).</li> <li><code>side</code> uses the trade-direction vocabulary (<code>BUY</code> / <code>SELL</code>) — distinct from the outcome-side vocabulary (<code>yes</code> / <code>no</code>) used by <code>/markets/{id}/trades</code>.</li> </ul>
- [Get wallet activity feed](https://docs.tatum.io/reference/getpredictionwalletactivityv4.md): <p style='display: none'>/v4/data/prediction/wallets/{address}/activity</p> <p><b>100 credits per API call</b></p>  <p>Full wallet event feed — covers every event type the upstream emits: <code>TRADE</code>, <code>SPLIT</code>, <code>MERGE</code>, <code>REDEEM</code>, <code>REWARD</code>, <code>CONVERSION</code>, <code>MAKER_REBATE</code> and <code>REFERRAL_REWARD</code>. Unlike <code>/wallets/{address}/trades</code>, this endpoint includes non-trade events and supports a native time-range filter — useful for activity logs, audit trails and tax exports.</p>  <p><b>Platform support</b></p> <ul> <li>Polymarket only. Kalshi wallets return <code>400</code>.</li> </ul>  <p><b>Filtering, sorting and pagination</b></p> <ul> <li><code>market</code> / <code>eventId</code> scope the response (mutually exclusive, up to 50 entries each).</li> <li><code>type</code> is a comma-separated list of event types — up to 8 entries from the enum.</li> <li><code>side</code> filters to <code>BUY</code> or <code>SELL</code> (relevant for trade-type rows; null on non-trade rows).</li> <li><code>start</code> / <code>end</code> are unix-<strong>seconds</strong> (not milliseconds) — values that look like millisecond timestamps (~13 digits) are rejected with <code>400</code>. <code>end</code> must be greater than or equal to <code>start</code>.</li> <li><code>sortBy</code> accepts <code>TIMESTAMP</code>, <code>TOKENS</code>, <code>CASH</code>. Defaults to <code>TIMESTAMP</code>. Combine with <code>sortDirection</code> (defaults to <code>DESC</code>).</li> <li>Pagination is offset-based — <code>limit</code> (1..500, default 100) and <code>offset</code> (0..10000, default 0).</li> </ul>  <p><b>Other notes</b></p> <ul> <li><code>side</code> is <code>null</code> on non-trade rows (e.g. <code>REWARD</code>, <code>SPLIT</code>, <code>MERGE</code>, <code>REDEEM</code>) — direction is undefined there.</li> <li><code>outcomeIndex</code> can be <code>null</code> for rows that don't map to a position side. A sentinel value of <code>999</code> is occasionally emitted by the upstream for the same case.</li> <li><code>conditionId</code> and <code>asset</code> can be <code>null</code> on rows that aren't market-scoped (e.g. some reward / referral rows).</li> <li><code>address</code> is normalised to lowercase before being echoed in the response.</li> </ul>
- [Get wallet open-position notional](https://docs.tatum.io/reference/getpredictionwalletvaluev4.md): <p style='display: none'>/v4/data/prediction/wallets/{address}/value</p> <p><b>50 credits per API call</b></p>  <p>Total USD notional of a wallet's currently open positions. A single-number endpoint — useful for sparkline-style summaries and headline KPIs without paying the cost of pulling the full positions list.</p>  <p><b>Platform support</b></p> <ul> <li>Polymarket only. Kalshi wallets return <code>400</code>.</li> </ul>  <p><b>Filtering</b></p> <ul> <li><code>market</code> scopes the total to a comma-separated list of condition ids (<code>0x</code> + 64 hex), up to 50 entries. Omit for the wallet-wide total.</li> </ul>  <p><b>Other notes</b></p> <ul> <li><code>value</code> is rounded to 2 decimal places.</li> <li><code>user</code> is the lowercased address.</li> </ul>
- [Get wallet markets-traded count](https://docs.tatum.io/reference/getpredictionwalletmarketstradedv4.md): <p style='display: none'>/v4/data/prediction/wallets/{address}/markets-traded</p> <p><b>50 credits per API call</b></p>  <p>Count of distinct markets a wallet has ever traded. Lifetime metric — useful as a quick activity signal alongside <code>/wallets/{address}/value</code> without paying the cost of pulling the full trade history.</p>  <p><b>Platform support</b></p> <ul> <li>Polymarket only. Kalshi wallets return <code>400</code>.</li> </ul>  <p><b>Other notes</b></p> <ul> <li><code>traded</code> is an integer count — the number of distinct markets, not the number of trades.</li> <li><code>user</code> is the lowercased address.</li> </ul>
- [Get top traders leaderboard](https://docs.tatum.io/reference/getpredictiontoptradersv4.md): <p style='display: none'>/v4/data/prediction/top-traders</p> <p><b>200 credits per API call</b></p>  <p>Ranked leaderboard of top traders, scoped by category and time period. Each row carries the trader's wallet, display fields (username, X handle, profile image), verified-badge status, and the metrics used for ranking (volume and PnL within the requested window).</p>  <p><b>Platform support</b></p> <ul> <li>Polymarket only.</li> </ul>  <p><b>Filtering, sorting and pagination</b></p> <ul> <li><code>category</code> selects the leaderboard slice — one of <code>OVERALL</code>, <code>POLITICS</code>, <code>SPORTS</code>, <code>CRYPTO</code>, <code>CULTURE</code>, <code>MENTIONS</code>, <code>WEATHER</code>, <code>ECONOMICS</code>, <code>TECH</code>, <code>FINANCE</code>. Defaults to <code>OVERALL</code>.</li> <li><code>timePeriod</code> sets the lookback window — <code>DAY</code>, <code>WEEK</code>, <code>MONTH</code>, <code>ALL</code>. Defaults to <code>DAY</code>.</li> <li><code>orderBy</code> chooses the ranking metric: <code>PNL</code> (realised + unrealised profit) or <code>VOL</code> (trading volume). Defaults to <code>PNL</code>.</li> <li><code>user</code> filters the leaderboard to a single wallet — useful for "where does this trader rank" lookups. <code>userName</code> filters by trader display name (substring match).</li> <li>Pagination is offset-based — <code>limit</code> (1..50, default 25) and <code>offset</code> (0..1000, default 0). Higher offsets are not supported by the upstream leaderboard.</li> </ul>  <p><b>Other notes</b></p> <ul> <li><code>rank</code> is the continuous 1-indexed position given the requested filters — <code>rank = 1</code> is the leader for the chosen category and time period.</li> <li><code>proxyWallet</code> is returned lowercased.</li> <li><code>vol</code> and <code>pnl</code> are USD figures, scoped to the requested <code>category</code> and <code>timePeriod</code>.</li> </ul>
- [Unified search (events / tags / profiles)](https://docs.tatum.io/reference/getpredictionsearchv4.md): <p style='display: none'>/v4/data/prediction/search</p> <p><b>50 credits per API call</b></p>  <p>Unified search across events, tags and trader profiles. One query returns all three result types in a single response — pick the branches you care about with <code>searchTags</code> and <code>searchProfiles</code>. Designed for discovery surfaces (autocomplete, top-of-page search) where a single query needs to surface the most relevant entities of any kind.</p>  <p><b>Platform support</b></p> <ul> <li>Polymarket only.</li> </ul>  <p><b>Filtering and pagination</b></p> <ul> <li><code>q</code> is the required search string — 2..128 characters. Single-character queries are rejected with <code>400</code> because they match an unhelpfully wide slice of the dataset.</li> <li><code>limitPerType</code> caps the number of results returned per branch (events, tags, profiles) — defaults to 10.</li> <li><code>searchTags</code> toggles the <code>tags</code> branch — defaults to <code>true</code>. <code>searchProfiles</code> toggles the <code>profiles</code> branch — defaults to <code>false</code> because most callers don't need it.</li> <li><code>eventsStatus</code> narrows the events branch by a free-text status (e.g. <code>active</code>); <code>eventsTag</code> is a comma-separated list of tag slugs (up to 50 entries). <code>keepClosedMarkets</code> overrides the default behaviour of hiding closed markets from the events branch.</li> <li><code>sort</code> is a free-text sort key forwarded to the upstream; <code>ascending</code> sets sort direction.</li> <li>Pagination is page-based (not cursor-based) — advance via <code>?page=N+1</code>. The response body carries <code>data.pagination.hasMore</code> and <code>data.pagination.totalResults</code> for paging decisions.</li> </ul>  <p><b>Other notes</b></p> <ul> <li>Branches are populated independently — an empty <code>tags</code> array means no tag matches were found, not that the search failed.</li> <li><code>events</code> share the same shape as the <code>/events</code> listing endpoint, so the same client-side rendering can be reused.</li> <li><code>profiles</code> rows include only <code>proxyWallet</code>, <code>name</code> and <code>pseudonym</code> — additional profile fields (image, bio) are not exposed here.</li> </ul>
- [Notifications Overview](https://docs.tatum.io/reference/notifications-overview.md): Our Notifications product makes on-chain alerts effortless. Simply create an alert for an address, and we’ll send you a webhook as soon as it triggers a transaction.
- [Supported Chains](https://docs.tatum.io/reference/notifications-supported-chains.md)
- [Create an alert](https://docs.tatum.io/reference/createsubscriptionv4.md): <p style='display: none'>/v4/subscription</p> <p><b>50 credits per sent Notification + 50 credits per day per alert</b></p>  <p>Create and manage blockchain event notification alerts. This endpoint allows you to receive real-time webhook alerts for address activity, token transfers, and native currency transactions across supported blockchains.</p>  <p><b>Tip:</b> Notifications are formatted using <b>Templates</b>. We recommend using the <b>enriched</b> template because it provides clean, human-readable enhanced data with token metadata. Learn more:   👉 <a href="https://docs.tatum.io/reference/createnotificationtemplate">Learn more</a></p>  <p><b>Supported Notification Types:</b></p> <ul>   <li><b>ADDRESS_EVENT</b> – Triggered for any address-level transaction or event.</li>   <li><b>INCOMING_FUNGIBLE_TX / OUTGOING_FUNGIBLE_TX</b> – Detects ERC20 or equivalent token transfers.</li>   <li><b>INCOMING_NATIVE_TX / OUTGOING_NATIVE_TX</b> – Detects native currency transfers (ETH, MATIC, etc.).</li> </ul>  <p>For more alert types &amp; chain support, see: 👉 <a href="https://docs.tatum.io/reference/notifications-supported-chains">Alerts Overview</a></p>  <p><b>Restrictions</b></p> <p>Each alert must be unique within your API key based on the following fields. If a matching alert already exists, creation will be rejected:</p> <ul>   <li><b>Address alerts</b> (ADDRESS_EVENT, INCOMING/OUTGOING_NATIVE_TX, etc.) – unique by <code>type + chain + address + url</code></li>   <li><b>Contract log alerts</b> (CONTRACT_ADDRESS_LOG_EVENT) – unique by <code>type + chain + contractAddress + event + url</code></li>   <li><b>Block alerts</b> (FAILED_TXS_PER_BLOCK) – unique by <code>type + chain + url</code></li> </ul> <p>To monitor the same address with different configurations (e.g. different conditions or finality), use a different webhook URL for each alert.</p>  <p><b>Webhook Response Format</b></p> <p><i>Recommended Default: <code>templateId = "enriched"</code></i></p>  <p>If you do not specify a custom template, the response will use the legacy format. However, the <b>enriched</b> template is recommended because it provides structured, human-readable fields and token metadata.</p>  <p>To learn about legacy format, custom templates, or <code>enriched_with_raw_data</code>, visit:   👉 <a href="https://docs.tatum.io/reference/createnotificationtemplate">Learn more</a></p>  <p><b>Example: Enriched Template Webhook Payload</b></p> <pre>   {     "data": {       "kind": "transfer",       "blockHash": "0x1234567890abcdef...",       "blockNumber": 18500000,       "blockTimestamp": 1699123456,       "txId": "0xabcdef1234567890...",       "currency": "ETH",       "txTimestamp": 1699123456,       "from": "0x742d35Cc6634C0532925a3b8D4C9db96C4b4d8b6",       "to": "0x8ba1f109551bD432803012645Hac136c",       "value": "1000000000000000000",        "contractAddress": "0xA0b86a33E6441b8c4C8C0C8C0C8C0C8C0C8C0C8C0C",       "tokenId": "12345",        "additionalData": {         "gasUsed": "21000",         "gasPrice": "20000000000"       },        "tokenMetadata": {         "type": "nft",         "decimals": 0,         "symbol": "NFT",         "name": "My NFT",         "uri": "https://api.example.com/metadata/12345"       },        "subscriptionId": "64f1a2b3c4d5e6f7g8h9i0j1",       "subscriptionType": "ADDRESS_EVENT"     },     "location": "...",     "scheme": "..."   } </pre>
- [List all active alerts](https://docs.tatum.io/reference/getsubscriptionsv4.md): <p style="display: none">/v4/subscription</p> <p>List all active alerts.</p>

To use it with v3, simply replace 'v4' with 'v3' in the request URL.  <p style="display: none">/v4/subscription/count</p> <p>Count of alerts that were found from /v4/subscription</p>

- [Create an alert](https://docs.tatum.io/reference/createsubscription.md): > 📘 **Tip:** To create Notifications on the mainnet or testnet you can use your respective keys from the Dashboard.  <p style="display: none">/v3/subscription</p> <p><b>50 credits per sent Notification + 50 credits per day per alert</b></p> <p>For Free plans, there is a monthly limit of sent webhooks, which is <b>1000</b>.</p> <p>If the webhook is sent unsuccessfully, it will be retried. The number of retries depends on the plan. </p> <ul>   <li><b>Free Plan</b> - 3 retries</li>   <li><b>Paid plans</b> - 10 retries</li> </ul> <p>Create an alert as an HTTP web hook.</p> <p>The following alert types are available:</p> <ul> <li><b>ADDRESS_TRANSACTION</b> - Enable HTTP POST JSON notifications for any blockchain transaction (both incoming and outgoing) at a specified address. This notification applies to transactions in the native blockchain currency or with any type of blockchain tokens.<br/> - For <b>EVM-based blockchains</b> (ETH), this web hook might be first invoked when a transaction appears in the mempool. In that case the webhook has a field "mempool" set to "true". Then it is invoked again once the transaction is added to a block.<br/> - For the <b>other blockchains</b>, this webhook is invoked when a transaction is added to a block.<br/> Free community plans can monitor up to 10 addresses per plan.<br/> <p>The following table describes the availability of this notification type on different blockchains and the credit consumption:</p> <table>   <tr>     <th>Chain</th>     <th>Testnet/Mainnet</th>     <th>Token assets support</th>     <th>Plan limitation</th>   </tr>   <tr>     <td>Solana</td>     <td>Yes/Yes</td>     <td>SOL, SPL and NFTs</td>     <td>Free plans - 10 addresses across all blockchains, Paid plans - unlimited addresses across all blockchains</td>   </tr>   <tr>     <td>Polygon</td>     <td>Yes/Yes</td>     <td>MATIC, ERC20, ERC721, ERC1155</td>     <td>Free plans - 10 addresses across all blockchains, Paid plans - unlimited addresses across all blockchains</td>   </tr>   <tr>     <td>Bitcoin</td>     <td>Yes/Yes</td>     <td>BTC</td>     <td>Free plans - 10 addresses across all blockchains, Paid plans - unlimited addresses across all blockchains</td>   </tr>   <tr>     <td>Litecoin</td>     <td>Yes/Yes</td>     <td>LTC</td>     <td>Free plans - 10 addresses across all blockchains, Paid plans - unlimited addresses across all blockchains</td>   </tr>   <tr>     <td>Bitcoin Cash</td>     <td>Yes/Yes</td>     <td>BCH, only incoming transactions</td>     <td>Free plans - 10 addresses across all blockchains, Paid plans - unlimited addresses across all blockchains</td>   </tr>   <tr>     <td>Dogecoin</td>     <td>Yes/Yes</td>     <td>DOGE, only incoming transactions</td>     <td>Free plans - 10 addresses across all blockchains, Paid plans - unlimited addresses across all blockchains</td>   </tr>    <tr>     <td>Tron</td>     <td>Yes/Yes</td>     <td>Tron, TRC10/TRC20</td>     <td>Free plans - 10 addresses across all blockchains, Paid plans - unlimited addresses across all blockchains</td>   </tr>   <tr>     <td>Binance Smart Chain</td>     <td>Yes/Yes</td>     <td>BSC, BEP20, ERC721, ERC1155</td>     <td>Free plans - 10 addresses across all blockchains, Paid plans - unlimited addresses across all blockchains</td>   </tr>   <tr>     <td>Other chains</td>     <td>Yes/Yes</td>     <td>NATIVE, ERC20, ERC721, ERC1155</td>     <td>Free plans - 10 addresses across all blockchains, Paid plans - unlimited addresses across all blockchains</td>   </tr>      </table> The request body of the POST request is a JSON object with the following structure:<br/> <pre>{   "address": "FykfMwA9WNShzPJbbb9DNXsfgDgS3XZzWiFgrVXfWoPJ", // the address on which the transaction occurs; for EVM-based chains, this is the sender's address   "txId": "2rdy3YCZHSwvpWtuDom1d4Jjy5UU9STLxF3ffXau6GToReDkfw8wEgX541fvzvh6btVC5D8iNapcKTXfPsoDBk7A", // the transaction ID   "blockNumber": 110827114, // the block number; does not appear if the transaction is in the mempool (for EVM-based blockchains)   "asset": "3gUeeR3BfVhukYJMwtHownRtRkGcf1bvwiV8TbKMZBVz", // the asset of the transaction: for token assets, this is the token address; for native blochckain assets, this is the name of the asset (for example, SOL)   "amount": "1", // the amount of the asset that was credited to (+) or debited from (-) the address; for EVM-based chains, when "counterAddress" is present, the amount is always positive   "tokenId": "1", // (ERC-721 / ERC-1155 only) the ID of the transferred token   "type": "token", // the type of the transaction; can be either "native" or "token"   "mempool": true, // (EVM-based blockchains only) if appears and set to "true", the transaction is in the mempool; if set to "false" or does not appear at all, the transaction has been added to a block   "counterAddress": undefined // an optional counter party address of the transaction; for EVM-based blockchains, this is the recipient's address // UTXO based chains return without this parameter, since UTXO is many-to-many by design.   "addressesRiskRatio": [ // (optional, subject to change; for Solana mainnet only) risk levels assigned to the addresses with which the address communicated within the transaction; the addresses are assessed using the AML/CFT solution by blockmate.io, see https://docs.blockmate.io/docs/risk-API/sources     {"vTEfAhXTmvgFmepgfhzBbRrJ4EtUP9adbMJjpzLsDMk": 0},     {"zBbRrJ4EtUP9adbMJjpzLsDMkvTEfAhXTmvgFmepgfh": 100}   ] }</pre></li> <li><b>CONTRACT_NFT_TXS_PER_BLOCK</b> - Enable HTTP POST JSON notifications for any event of minting, transferring, or burning an NFT that was emitted from any smart contract. This notification is fired as a batch notification per block: it includes all NFT minting, transferring, or burning events from all smart contracts that got recorded into a block.<br/> Free community plans can monitor 1 event across all blockchains per plan.<br/><br/> The request body of the POST request is a JSON object with the following structure:<br/> <pre>{  "events": [    {      "timestamp": 1661961758000,      "from": "0x0000000000000000000000000000000000000000",      "to": "0xfa4e7035b34294407e5df1603215983d65e5a773",      "tokenId": "14671",      "txId": "0x916fcf878656f7a3772317697e6d2740ac8b1b7dbe6d029aa79592fd72f3fb0b",      "contractAddress": "0x55a2430e32dcebc3649120f26f917d1f0686f74c",      "type": "erc721",      "amount": "1",      "logIndex": 578,      "metadataURI": "https://graphigo.prd.galaxy.eco/metadata/0x55a2430e32dcebc3649120f26f917d1f0686f74c/14671.json"    }  ],  "blockNumber": 32522207,  "chain": "polygon-mainnet",  "subscriptionType": "CONTRACT_NFT_TXS_PER_BLOCK"  }</pre></li> <li><b>CONTRACT_MULTITOKEN_TXS_PER_BLOCK</b> - Enable HTTP POST JSON notifications for any event of minting, transferring, or burning a Multi-Token that was emitted from any smart contract. This notification is fired as a batch notification per block: it includes all Multi Token minting, transferring, or burning events from all smart contracts that got recorded into a block.<br/> Free community plans can monitor 1 event across all blockchains per plan.<br/><br/> The request body of the POST request is a JSON object with the following structure:<br/> <pre>{  "events": [    {      "timestamp": 1661961758000,      "from": "0x85bd970cf0e49acd572f9c2d608713bc55b5ee0e",      "to": "0x9ab31230d5413e4af01af9bb697651a3c70c95c9",      "tokenId": "1450000023306",      "txId": "0x565b9ca6b7b5ff1b1ce0725acd785dbf5ff0930d5ff547bad044c0960291555f",      "contractAddress": "0xdb6290f355a528a7284bdd528b05a78fb05226ca",      "type": "erc1155",      "amount": "1",      "logIndex": 571,      "metadataURI": "https://touhao.bj.bcebos.com/nft/metadata/1450000023306.json"    }  ],  "blockNumber": 32522207,  "chain": "polygon-mainnet",  "subscriptionType": "CONTRACT_MULTITOKEN_TXS_PER_BLOCK" }</pre></li> <li><b>CONTRACT_LOG_EVENT</b> - Enable HTTP POST JSON notifications for any event that was emitted from any smart contract. This notification is fired as a batch notification per block: it includes all events from all smart contracts that got recorded into a block.<br/> Free community plans can monitor 1 event across all blockchains per plan.<br/><br/> The request body of the POST request is a JSON object with the following structure:<br/> <pre>{  "events": [    {      "txId": "0xc98307f09ed527d5cff8305e8f65226b790e3317ded10b9e58f6f07286dcf8f1",      "logIndex": 326,      "timestamp": 1661774265000,      "address": "0xc2132d05d31c914a87c6611c10748aeb04b58e8f",      "topic_0": "0xddf252ad1be2c89b69c2b068fc378daa952ba7f163c4a11628f55a4df523b3ef",      "topic_1": "0x000000000000000000000000a91ab7d77892a559d2a95baaf1d748fc97c65d29",      "topic_2": "0x0000000000000000000000009b08288c3be4f62bbf8d1c20ac9c5e6f9467d8b7",      "topic_3": null,      "data": "0x0000000000000000000000000000000000000000000000000000000002c9e3e4"    }  ],  "blockNumber": 32447538,  "chain": "polygon-amoy",  "subscriptionType": "CONTRACT_LOG_EVENT" }</pre></li> <li><b>ACCOUNT_INCOMING_BLOCKCHAIN_TRANSACTION</b> - Enable HTTP POST JSON notifications about incoming blockchain transactions on virtual accounts. This web hook is invoked when an incoming transaction is reflected on the balance of the virtual account (the balance is credited with the transaction amount). This happens when the transaction has the sufficient number of blockchain confirmations: two confirmations for BTC, LTC, BCH, and DOGE, and one confirmation for the other blockchains.<br/> The request body of the POST request is a JSON object with the following structure: <pre>{   "date": 1619176527481,   "amount": "0.005",   "currency": "BTC",   "subscriptionType":"ACCOUNT_INCOMING_BLOCKCHAIN_TRANSACTION",   "accountId": "6082ab462936b4478117c6a0",   "reference: "c9875708-4ba3-41c9-a4cd-271048b41b9a", // the reference of the transaction in the virtual account   "txId": "45af182a0ffab58e5ba32fee57b297b2260c6e23a1de5ddc76c7ee22d72dea99",   "blockHash": "45af182a0ffab58e5ba32fee57b297b2260c6e23a1de5ddc76c7ee22d72dea99", // the hash of the block, might not be present every time   "blockHeight": 12345,   "from": "SENDER_ADDRESS", // might not be present every time; not present for UTXO-based blockchains   "to": "RECIPIENT_ADDRESS_CONNECTED_TO_LEDGER_ACCOUNT", // the blockchain address of the recipient   "index": 5 // for UTXO-based blockchains (BCH, BTC, DOGE, LTC), this is the index of the output in the transaction }</pre></li> <li><b>ACCOUNT_PENDING_BLOCKCHAIN_TRANSACTION</b> - Enable HTTP POST JSON notifications about incoming blockchain transactions on virtual accounts.<br /> This web hook is supported only for BCH, BTC, DOGE, ETH, and LTC accounts.<br /> - For <b>BTC-based blockchains</b> (BCH, BTC, DOGE, and LTC), this web hook is invoked when an incoming transaction appears in a block for the first time. At that time, the transaction has one confirmation but this is not enough for the transaction to be reflected on the balance of the virtual account yet. Instead, a deposit corresponding to the pending transaction with a status of "in progress" appears on the virtual account. Once the transaction is added to the block, the deposit's status changes to "done", and the account balance gets updated.<br /> - For <b>EVM-based blockchains</b> (ETH), this web hook is invoked when an incoming transaction appears in the mempool. The virtual account balance is not updated until the transaction is added to a block. Instead, a deposit corresponding to the pending transaction with a status of "in progress" appears on the virtual account. Once the transaction is added to the block, the deposit's status changes to "done", and the account balance gets updated.<br /> The request body of the POST request is a JSON object with the following structure:<br/> <pre>{   "date": 1619176527481,   "amount": "0.005",   "currency": "BTC",   "accountId": "6082ab462936b4478117c6a0",   "reference: "c9875708-4ba3-41c9-a4cd-271048b41b9a", // the reference of the transaction in the virtual account   "txId": "45af182a0ffab58e5ba32fee57b297b2260c6e23a1de5ddc76c7ee22d72dea99",   "blockHash": "45af182a0ffab58e5ba32fee57b297b2260c6e23a1de5ddc76c7ee22d72dea99", // the hash of the block, might not be present every time; if set to "null", the transaction is in the mempool (for EVM-based blockchains)   "blockHeight": 12345, // if set to "null", the transaction is in the mempool (for EVM-based blockchains)   "from": "SENDER_ADDRESS", // might not be present every time; not present for UTXO-based blockchains (BCH, BTC, DOGE, LTC)   "to": "RECIPIENT_ADDRESS_CONNECTED_TO_LEDGER_ACCOUNT", // the blockchain address of the recipient   "index": 5 // for UTXO-based blockchains (BCH, BTC, DOGE, LTC), this is the index of the output in the transaction }</pre></li> <li><b>CUSTOMER_TRADE_MATCH</b> - Enable HTTP POST JSON notifications on closed trade, which occurs on any customer account. This web hook will be invoked, when the open trade is filled and closed. Works also for the Trade Futures. If is triggered by the futures, bool field expiredWithoutMatch is present. Request body of the POST request will be JSON object with attributes:<br/> <pre>{   "created": 1619176527481,   "amount": "0.005",   "price": "0.02",   "type": "SELL",   "pair": "VC_CHF/VC_CHF3",   "id": "6082ab462936b4478117c6a0", // id of the trade   "currency1AccountId": "6082ab462936b4478117c6a0",   "currency2AccountId": "6082ab512936b4478117c6a2",   "fee": null,   "feeAccountId": null,   "isMaker": true,   "expiredWithoutMatch": false }</pre></li> <li><b>CUSTOMER_PARTIAL_TRADE_MATCH</b> - Enable HTTP POST JSON notifications on partialy filled trade, which occurs on any customer account. This web hook will be invoked, when the open trade is partialy filled. Request body of the POST request will be JSON object with attributes:<br/> <pre>{   "created": 1619176527481,   "amount": "0.005",   "orderAmount": "0.1",   "price": "0.02",   "type": "SELL",   "pair": "VC_CHF/VC_CHF3",   "id": "6082ab462936b4478117c6a0", // id of the trade   "currency1AccountId": "6082ab462936b4478117c6a0",   "currency2AccountId": "6082ab512936b4478117c6a2",   "fee": null,   "feeAccountId": null,   "isMaker": true,   "expiredWithoutMatch": false }</pre></li> <li><b>TRANSACTION_IN_THE_BLOCK</b> - Enable HTTP POST JSON notifications on ledger => blockchain transaction, when transaction is included in the block. This web hook will be invoked, when the outgoing transaction is included in the block. Request body of the POST request will be JSON object with attributes:<br/> <pre>   {     "txId": "0x026f4f05b972c09279111da13dfd20d8df04eff436d7f604cd97b9ffaa690567",     "reference": "90270634-5b07-4fad-b17b-f82899953533",     "accountId": "6086ed0744c45b24d4fbd039",     "currency": "BSC",     "withdrawalId": "608fe5b73a893234ba379ab2",     "address": "0x8ce4e40889a13971681391AAd29E88eFAF91f784",     "amount": "0.1",     "blockHeight": 8517664   }</pre></li> <li><b>KMS_FAILED_TX</b> - Enable HTTP POST JSON notifications on error during KMS signature process. This web hook will be invoked, when the Tatum KMS receives error during processing transactions. Request body of the POST request will be JSON object with attributes:<br/> <pre>{   "signatureId": "6082ab462936b4478117c6a0",   "error": "Error message from the KMS" }</pre></li> <li><b>KMS_COMPLETED_TX</b> - Enable HTTP POST JSON notifications on successful completion of KMS signature process. This web hook will be invoked, when the Tatum KMS successfully completes the signature during processing transactions. Request body of the POST request will be JSON object with attributes:<br/> <pre>{   "signatureId": "6082ab462936b4478117c6a0",   "txId": "0x7bb7d3b90567e89f999f2e3d263bc3738a018dbbcfa9f5397678cf17cdf0235f" }</pre></li> </ul> In case of unsuccesful web hook response status - other then 2xx - web hook is repeated 9 more times with exponential backoff. Parameters are T = 15 * 2.7925^9, where 15 is interval in s, backoff rate is 2.7925 and 9 is current number of retries. Last web hook is fired after 24 hours approximatelly. After last failed attempt, web hook is deleted from our system. The 2xx response must be returned in 10 seconds after web hook is fired.<br/> Result of the operation is an alert ID, which can be used to cancel the alert or obtain additional data connected to it like reports.</p>

- [Enable HMAC webhook digest](https://docs.tatum.io/reference/enablewebhookhmac.md): > This endpoint is compatible with our deprecated v3 Notifications product. To use it with v3, simply replace 'v4' with 'v3' in the request URL.  <p style="display: none">/v4/subscription</p> <p>Enable HMAC hash ID on the fired webhooks from Tatum API. In order to make sure that a webhook is sent by us, we have the possibility to sign it with the HMAC Sha512 Hex algorithm.<br/> To verify that a webhook is sent by us <ol> <li>Get a webhook <b>x-payload-hash</b> header value and payload as it is as a JSON file.</li> <li>Convert the HTTP webhook body to stringify JSON without any spaces. In JavaScript, you would do it like this <pre>JSON.stringify(req.body)</pre></li> <li>Perform calculations on your side to create a digest using Secret Key, webhook payload in bytes and HMAC SHA512 algorithm. JavaScript example: <pre>require('crypto').createHmac('sha512', hmacSecret).update(JSON.stringify(req.body)).digest('base64')</pre>.</li> <li>Compare x-payload-hash header value with calculated digest as a Base64 string.</li></p>
- [Disable HMAC webhook digest](https://docs.tatum.io/reference/disablewebhookhmac.md): > This endpoint is compatible with our deprecated v3 Notifications product. To use it with v3, simply replace 'v4' with 'v3' in the request URL.  <p style="display: none">/v4/subscription</p> <p>Disable HMAC hash ID on the fired webhooks from Tatum API.</p>

You can check al available APIs here - <a href="https://developer.algorand.org/docs/rest-apis/algod/v2/" target="_blank">https://developer.algorand.org/docs/rest-apis/algod/v2/</a>.  <br/>  Example call for Broadcast a raw transaction is described in the response. <a href="https://developer.algorand.org/docs/rest-apis/algod/v2/#post-v2transactions" target="_blank">https://developer.algorand.org/docs/rest-apis/algod/v2/#post-v2transactions</a>.  <br/>  URL used for this call would be <pre>https://api.tatum.io/v3/algorand/node/algod/YOUR_API_KEY/v2/transactions</pre>  </p>

- [Send Algos to an Algorand account](https://docs.tatum.io/reference/algorandblockchaintransfer.md): <p><b>2 credits per API call</b></p> <p>Send Algos from one Algorand address to the other one.</p> <p><b>Signing a transaction</b></p> <p>When sending Algos, you are charged a fee for the transaction, and you must sign the transaction with the private key of the blockchain address from which the fee will be deducted.</p> <p>Providing the private key in the API is not a secure way of signing transactions, because the private key can be stolen or exposed. Your private keys should never leave your security perimeter. You should use the private keys only for testing a solution you are building on the <b>testnet</b> of a blockchain.</p> <p>For signing transactions on the <b>mainnet</b>, we strongly recommend that you use the Tatum <a href="https://github.com/tatumio/tatum-kms" target="_blank">Key Management System (KMS)</a> and provide the signature ID instead of the private key in the API. Alternatively, you can use the <a href="https://github.com/tatumio/tatum-js/tree/v2" target="_blank">Tatum JavaScript client</a>.</p>
- [Enable receiving asset on account](https://docs.tatum.io/reference/algorandblockchainreceiveasset.md): <h4>2 credits per API call.</h4><br/> <p>Enable accepting Algorand asset on the sender account.<br/><br/> This operation needs the private key of the blockchain address. No one should ever send it's own private keys to the internet because there is a strong possibility of stealing keys and loss of funds. In this method, it is possible to enter privateKey or signatureId. PrivateKey should be used only for quick development on testnet versions of blockchain when there is no risk of losing funds. In production, <a href="https://github.com/tatumio/tatum-kms" target="_blank">Tatum KMS</a> should be used for the highest security standards, and signatureId should be present in the request. Alternatively, using the Tatum client library for supported languages. </p>

- [Generate Arb wallet](https://docs.tatum.io/reference/arbgeneratewallet.md): <p style="display: none">/v3/arb/wallet</p> <h4>1 credit per API call.</h4><br/><p>Tatum supports BIP44 HD wallets. Because they can generate 2^31 addresses from 1 mnemonic phrase, they are very convenient and secure. A mnemonic phrase consists of 24 special words in a defined order and can restore access to all generated addresses and private keys.<br/>Each address is identified by 3 main values:<ul><li>Private Key - your secret value which should never be revealed</li><li>Public Key - a public address to be published</li><li>Derivation index - an index of generated address</li></ul></p><p>Tatum follows the BIP44 specification and generates for Arb wallets with the derivation path m/44'/60'/0'/0. More about BIP44 HD wallets can be found here - <a target="_blank" href="https://github.com/bitcoin/bips/blob/master/bip-0044.mediawiki">https://github.com/bitcoin/bips/blob/master/bip-0044.mediawiki</a>. Generates a BIP44 compatible Arb wallet.</p>
- [Generate Arb account address from Extended public key](https://docs.tatum.io/reference/arbgenerateaddress.md): <p style="display: none">/v3/arb/address/{xpub}/{index}</p> <h4>1 credit per API call.</h4><br/> <p>Generates an Arb account deposit address from an Extended public key. The deposit address is generated for the specific index - each extended public key can generate up to 2^31 addresses starting from index 0 until 2^31 - 1.</p>
- [Generate Arb private key](https://docs.tatum.io/reference/arbgenerateaddressprivatekey.md): <p style="display: none">/v3/arb/wallet/priv</p> <h4>1 credit per API call.</h4><br/> <p>Generates the private key of an address from a mnemonic for a given derivation path index. The private key is generated for the specific index - each mnemonic can generate up to 2^32 private keys starting from index 0 until 2^31 - 1.</p>

- [Invoke a method in a smart contract on Avalanche](https://docs.tatum.io/reference/avalancheblockchainsmartcontractinvocation.md): <p style="display: none">/v3/avalanche/smartcontract</p> <p><b>2 credits per API call</b></p> <p>Invoke a method in an existing smart contract on Avalanche.</p> <p>You can call a read-only or write method.</p> <ul> <li>For <b>read-only</b> methods, the output of the invoked method is returned.</li> <li>For <b>write</b> methods, the ID of the associated transaction is returned.</li> </ul> <p><b>Troubleshooting a failed transaction</b><br/> Tatum ensures that this API works against the blockchain (accesses the blockchain, finds the specified smart contract, and executes the specified ABI method with the provided parameters).<br/>However, because this API can be run against any smart contract on the blockchain, Tatum cannot in any way guarantee that the method itself will be executed successfully.</p> <p>If you have issues with invoking the method, refer to the user documentation for this method, or contact the author of the smart contract.</p> <p>For more information about invoking methods in smart contracts, see <a href="https://support.tatum.io/support/solutions/articles/80001052441" target="_blank">this article</a> on our Support Portal.</p> <p><b>Signing a transaction</b><br/> When invoking a method in a smart contract, you are charged a fee for the transaction, and you must sign the transaction with the private key of the blockchain address from which the fee will be deducted.</p> <p>If <b>caller</b> field is present instead of the private key, Tatum will sign the transaction with the managed private key connected to the caller address. This is applicable only for paid mainnet plans and all testnet plans. Keep in mind that the caller address must have enough access right to perform the action in the smart contract method.</p> <p>Providing the private key in the API is not a secure way of signing transactions, because the private key can be stolen or exposed. Your private keys should never leave your security perimeter. You should use the private keys only for testing a solution you are building on the <b>testnet</b> of a blockchain.</p> <p>For signing transactions on the <b>mainnet</b>, we strongly recommend that you use the Tatum <a href="https://github.com/tatumio/tatum-kms" target="_blank">Key Management System (KMS)</a> and provide the signature ID instead of the private key in the API. Alternatively, you can use the <a href="https://github.com/tatumio/tatum-js/tree/v2" target="_blank">Tatum JavaScript client</a>.</p>

- [eth_getproof](https://docs.tatum.io/reference/rpc-base-eth_getproof.md): Base RPC

- [Generate Base wallet](https://docs.tatum.io/reference/basegeneratewallet.md): <p style="display: none">/v3/base/wallet</p> <h4>1 credit per API call.</h4><br/><p>Tatum supports BIP44 HD wallets. Because they can generate 2^31 addresses from 1 mnemonic phrase, they are very convenient and secure. A mnemonic phrase consists of 24 special words in a defined order and can restore access to all generated addresses and private keys.<br/>Each address is identified by 3 main values:<ul><li>Private Key - your secret value which should never be revealed</li><li>Public Key - a public address to be published</li><li>Derivation index - an index of generated address</li></ul></p><p>Tatum follows the BIP44 specification and generates for Base wallets with the derivation path m/44'/60'/0'/0. More about BIP44 HD wallets can be found here - <a target="_blank" href="https://github.com/bitcoin/bips/blob/master/bip-0044.mediawiki">https://github.com/bitcoin/bips/blob/master/bip-0044.mediawiki</a>. Generates a BIP44 compatible Base wallet.</p>
- [Generate Base account address from Extended public key](https://docs.tatum.io/reference/basegenerateaddress.md): <p style="display: none">/v3/base/address/{xpub}/{index}</p> <h4>1 credit per API call.</h4><br/> <p>Generates an Base account deposit address from an Extended public key. The deposit address is generated for the specific index - each extended public key can generate up to 2^31 addresses starting from index 0 until 2^31 - 1.</p>
- [Generate Base private key](https://docs.tatum.io/reference/basegenerateaddressprivatekey.md): <p style="display: none">/v3/base/wallet/priv</p> <h4>1 credit per API call.</h4><br/> <p>Generates the private key of an address from a mnemonic for a given derivation path index. The private key is generated for the specific index - each mnemonic can generate up to 2^32 private keys starting from index 0 until 2^31 - 1.</p>
- [Web3 HTTP driver](https://docs.tatum.io/reference/baseweb3driver.md): <p style="display: none">/v3/base/web3/{xApiKey}</p> <p><b>2 credits per API call</b></p> <p><b>This endpoint is deprecated. Use the <a href="https://apidoc.tatum.io/tag/Node-RPC" target="_blank">HTTP-based JSON RPC driver</a> instead.</b></p><br/> <p>Use this endpoint URL as an http-based web3 driver to connect directly to the Base node provided by Tatum. To learn more about Base Web3, visit the <a href="https://base.network/" target="_blank">Base developers' guide</a>.</p>
- [Send BASE or fungible tokens (ERC-20) from account to account](https://docs.tatum.io/reference/baseblockchaintransfer.md): <p style="display: none">/v3/base/transaction</p> <p><b>2 credits per API call</b></p> <p>Send BASE or Tatum-supported fungible tokens (ERC-20) from account to account.</p> <p style="border:4px solid DeepSkyBlue;"><b>NOTE:</b> Sending the fungible tokens is supported only on the mainnet.</p> <p><b>Signing a transaction</b><br/> When sending BASE, you are charged a fee for the transaction, and you must sign the transaction with the private key of the blockchain address from which the fee will be deducted.</p> <p>Providing the private key in the API is not a secure way of signing transactions, because the private key can be stolen or exposed. Your private keys should never leave your security perimeter. You should use the private keys only for testing a solution you are building on the <b>testnet</b> of a blockchain.</p>
- [Invoke a method in a smart contract on Base](https://docs.tatum.io/reference/baseblockchainsmartcontractinvocation.md): <p style="display: none">/v3/base/smartcontract</p> <p><b>2 credits per API call</b></p> <p>Invoke a method in an existing smart contract on Base.</p> <p>You can call a read-only or write method.</p> <ul> <li>For <b>read-only</b> methods, the output of the invoked method is returned.</li> <li>For <b>write</b> methods, the ID of the associated transaction is returned.</li> </ul> <p><b>Troubleshooting a failed transaction</b><br/> Tatum ensures that this API works against the blockchain (accesses the blockchain, finds the specified smart contract, and executes the specified ABI method with the provided parameters).<br/>However, because this API can be run against any smart contract on the blockchain, Tatum cannot in any way guarantee that the method itself will be executed successfully.</p> <p>If you have issues with invoking the method, refer to the user documentation for this method, or contact the author of the smart contract.</p> <p>For more information about invoking methods in smart contracts, see <a href="https://support.tatum.io/support/solutions/articles/80001052441" target="_blank">this article</a> on our Support Portal.</p> <p><b>Signing a transaction</b><br/> When invoking a method in a smart contract, you are charged a fee for the transaction, and you must sign the transaction with the private key of the blockchain address from which the fee will be deducted.</p> <p>If <b>caller</b> field is present instead of the private key, Tatum will sign the transaction with the managed private key connected to the caller address. This is applicable only for paid mainnet plans and all testnet plans. Keep in mind that the caller address must have enough access right to perform the action in the smart contract method.</p> <p>Providing the private key in the API is not a secure way of signing transactions, because the private key can be stolen or exposed. Your private keys should never leave your security perimeter. You should use the private keys only for testing a solution you are building on the <b>testnet</b> of a blockchain.</p>
- [Broadcast signed Base transaction](https://docs.tatum.io/reference/basebroadcast.md): <p style="display: none">/v3/base/broadcast</p> <p><b>2 credits per API call</b></p> <p>Broadcast signed transaction to Base blockchain.</p>
